Like, we had this safety meeting at work recently, and I... I ended up raising my voice and yelling before I could even stop myself. It wasn't my best moment, and I'm honestly so embarrassed about it. \n\nNow the younger inspectors on the line act kind of scared to come ask me anything, and I just hate that I'm making them uncomfortable. I really want to be a good coworker, you know? Am I explaining that right? Is that the sort of thing I should be telling you?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any recurring thoughts that you can't seem to get out of your head lately?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess maybe a little bit? I don't want to make it sound like I'm going crazy or anything, because I'm really not. But... sometimes, if I hear a loud clank from one of the carts on the floor, or... or if I catch the smell of hot oil, it's just... \n\nIt's like I'm right back in that bay. I just see my coworker by the machine, and it feels like it's happening right in front of me all over again. It's just for a second, really. I usually shake it off pretty quick. Is that... is that normal? I mean, for someone who went through something upsetting? I just hope I'm not making too big a deal out of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding any places, people, or activities since the incident?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I suppose I have been changing my routine just a tiny bit. I hope that's alright to admit. I don't want to sound like I'm not doing my job or anything, but... I do kind of go out of my way now so I don't have to walk past the inspection bay. It just takes an extra minute or two to walk the long way around, so it's not a huge problem for anyone else, I promise. \n\nAnd, um, I really don't talk about the accident if I can help it. If people bring it up, I just try to quietly change the subject. I don't want to make anyone else uncomfortable, you know? \n\nOh, and... well, this is a little embarrassing, but I actually quit going to these local industrial-history talks that I used to really enjoy. It sounds so silly, but the pictures they show of the old machinery... they just kind of set me off now. I feel bad for missing them, but it's just easier to stay home. Is it okay that I'm avoiding those things? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information, doctor.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself being constantly on the lookout for potential dangers or threats, even when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Well... Like, waiting for a sudden noise, or checking to see who's walking up behind me. Well, um... I guess things have been a little harder lately, but I try really hard not to let it affect anyone else. I don't want to be a burden on my friends or my family, you know? 

I have... well, I've pulled back a bit from some things. I used to do community theater, which I loved, but... I just couldn't reliably get through the rehearsals anymore without needing to leave or take a break. So I stepped away, just so I wouldn't let the cast down. 

And at work... I hope this doesn't make me sound like a terrible employee, because I really do try my best... but I've missed quite a few shifts. And I guess I've lost my patience a few times with the new trainees. It's not their fault, I know that, but... they don't really trust me to mentor the younger inspectors anymore. 

I feel awful about it. I really want to be helpful and do a good job. I hope you don't think I'm just being difficult or lazy. Is it okay that I'm telling you all this? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.

Oh... um... well, I know they said it was a mechanical issue, and I really try not to make it all about me because he's the one who actually suffered, but... yes. I do. 

I just... I keep thinking that if I had pushed harder about the safety checks before we started, or... or if I had just stopped that run when it didn’t feel quite right to me... maybe he wouldn’t have gotten hurt so bad. I'm the inspector, you know? It's my job to catch those things, and I just feel so terrible that I didn't. 

I hope you don't think I'm being silly or just feeling sorry for myself. I really don't want to sound like a martyr or anything. But I just can't shake the feeling that I should have done more. Is it... is it normal to feel like I could have prevented it? I hope I'm explaining this okay, I just want to make sure I'm giving you the honest answer.

I don't want to make anyone else uncomfortable, you know? \n\nOh, and... well, this is a little embarrassing, but I actually quit going to these local industrial-history talks that I used to really enjoy. It sounds so silly, but the pictures they show of the old machinery... they just kind of set me off now. I feel bad for missing them, but it's just easier to stay home. Is it okay that I'm avoiding those things? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information, doctor.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself being constantly on the lookout for potential dangers or threats, even when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Well... I guess I do, a little bit. I mean, I try not to let it show, because I don't want people to think I'm paranoid or anything like that. But... even when I'm just in the break room, or, um, I sing in a choir and even at rehearsal, I just... \n\nI find myself watching the doors a lot. And I'm always listening, you know? Like, waiting for a sudden noise, or checking to see who's walking up behind me. It's silly, I know I'm perfectly safe in those places, but it's like... my body just won't believe it? \n\nI really try to keep it to myself so I don't bother anyone else with it. I hope that makes sense. Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're reliving the accident, like it's happening all over again, even when you're not thinking about it?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't think so. I mean, not really? \n\nLike I mentioned earlier, it really only happens if something sets it off, like that smell of the oil or a loud clank. But if I'm just sitting at home, or... or doing the dishes, it doesn't just happen completely out of the blue. I try very hard to keep my mind busy so I'm not just dwelling on it. \n\nI hope that's a good sign? I really don't want to make it sound worse than it is, and I want to make sure I'm giving you the accurate picture. But no, I haven't noticed it happening just completely on its own without something reminding me first. Is that... does that make sense? I hope I'm answering that right.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way since the accident?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess it's been quite a while now. I hope I'm not taking up too much of your time by still struggling with this. \n\nIt started right after the accident, you know, the one with the machinery after they switched our schedules to overnight. And, um... it's been about two years and four months since then. I really thought I would have just gotten over it by now, and I feel a little bad that I haven't. I've tried my best to just push through and be a good employee, but it just seems to linger. Is it normal for it to take this long? I hope you don't think I'm just not trying hard enough to move on.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any problems in your relationships with family or friends since the accident?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess things have been a little harder lately, but I try really hard not to let it affect anyone else. I don't want to be a burden on my friends or my family, you know? \n\nI have... well, I've pulled back a bit from some things. I used to do community theater, which I loved, but... I just couldn't reliably get through the rehearsals anymore without needing to leave or take a break. So I stepped away, just so I wouldn't let the cast down. \n\nAnd at work... I hope this doesn't make me sound like a terrible employee, because I really do try my best... but I've missed quite a few shifts. And I guess I've lost my patience a few times with the new trainees. It's not their fault, I know that, but... they don't really trust me to mentor the younger inspectors anymore. \n\nI feel awful about it. I really want to be helpful and do a good job. I hope you don't think I'm just being difficult or lazy. Is it okay that I'm telling you all this? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or responsible in any way for the accident that happened at work?\n\nPatient: Oh... um... well, I know they said it was a mechanical issue, and I really try not to make it all about me because he's the one who actually suffered, but... yes. I do. \n\nI just... I keep thinking that if I had pushed harder about the safety checks before we started, or... or if I had just stopped that run when it didn’t feel quite right to me... maybe he wouldn’t have gotten hurt so bad. I'm the inspector, you know? It's my job to catch those things, and I just feel so terrible that I didn't. \n\nI hope you don't think I'm being silly or just feeling sorry for myself. I really don't want to sound like a martyr or anything. But I just can't shake the feeling that I should have done more. Is it... is it normal to feel like I could have prevented it? I hope I'm explaining this okay, I just want to make sure I'm giving you the honest answer.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I wouldn't say I've lost interest. I hope I didn't give you the wrong impression earlier when I mentioned stepping away from the theater. I still really love it, and I still enjoy my other little hobbies, like tending to my garden and reading. \n\nIt's just... sometimes it's a bit harder to actually get out and do them right now, but the interest is definitely still there. I try very hard to keep up with the things I like because I know I shouldn't just sit around, and I really want to get back to normal. I haven't given up on my interests or anything like that, I promise. \n\nDoes that make sense? I just want to make sure I'm explaining it clearly so I'm giving you the right picture.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Oh, um... no, like I was just mentioning, I really haven't lost my interest in the things I love. I'm sorry if I didn't explain it well enough a second ago! I still find my gardening and my books very enjoyable... in my heart, I mean. 

Even if I can't always get out and do them as easily right now, I still care about them just as much as I used to. I really try my hardest to stay positive and keep up with my life, I promise. I don't want you to think I've just given up.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, um... well, I really try not to let it affect my work, I promise I do. I want to be a good employee and a good person to be around. But... I suppose it has been a little difficult lately. I hope you don't think less of me, but... I've missed a few shifts. Well, maybe more than a few. 

And, um, I used to mentor the younger inspectors, which I loved, but... I guess I've lost my patience a bit with the trainees lately. They don't really trust me to do it right now, which is completely understandable, I know I haven't been myself. 

And I mentioned the theater earlier... I actually had to pull back from that because I just couldn't reliably get through the rehearsals without, you know, needing to leave or getting upset. I feel terrible about letting people down. I'm really trying to get back on track, I don't want to be a burden to anyone. Does that... does that answer your question? I hope I'm not sounding too negative, I just want to be honest with you.

I really appreciate it. \n\nUm... well, I guess I'm here because I've been a little bit... on edge lately? I hope it's okay to start with that. I've just been, well, snapping at people a little bit. It wasn't my best moment, and I'm honestly so embarrassed about it. \n\nNow the younger inspectors on the line act kind of scared to come ask me anything, and I just hate that I'm making them uncomfortable. I really want to be a good coworker, you know? Am I explaining that right? Is that the sort of thing I should be telling you?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any recurring thoughts that you can't seem to get out of your head lately?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess maybe a little bit? I don't want to make it sound like I'm going crazy or anything, because I'm really not. But... sometimes, if I hear a loud clank from one of the carts on the floor, or... or if I catch the smell of hot oil, it's just... \n\nIt's like I'm right back in that bay. I just see my coworker by the machine, and it feels like it's happening right in front of me all over again. It's just for a second, really. I usually shake it off pretty quick. Is that... is that normal? I mean, for someone who went through something upsetting? I just hope I'm not making too big a deal out of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding any places, people, or activities since the incident?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I suppose I have been changing my routine just a tiny bit. If people bring it up, I just try to quietly change the subject. I don't want to make anyone else uncomfortable, you know? \n\nOh, and... well, this is a little embarrassing, but I actually quit going to these local industrial-history talks that I used to really enjoy. It sounds so silly, but the pictures they show of the old machinery... they just kind of set me off now. I feel bad for missing them, but it's just easier to stay home. Is it okay that I'm avoiding those things? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information, doctor.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself being constantly on the lookout for potential dangers or threats, even when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Well... I guess I do, a little bit. I mean, I try not to let it show, because I don't want people to think I'm paranoid or anything like that. But... even when I'm just in the break room, or, um, I sing in a choir and even at rehearsal, I just... \n\nI find myself watching the doors a lot. And I'm always listening, you know? Like, waiting for a sudden noise, or checking to see who's walking up behind me. It's silly, I know I'm perfectly safe in those places, but it's like... my body just won't believe it? \n\nI really try to keep it to myself so I don't bother anyone else with it. I hope that makes sense. Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're reliving the accident, like it's happening all over again, even when you're not thinking about it?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't think so. I mean, not really? \n\nLike I mentioned earlier, it really only happens if something sets it off, like that smell of the oil or a loud clank. But if I'm just sitting at home, or... or doing the dishes, it doesn't just happen completely out of the blue. I try very hard to keep my mind busy so I'm not just dwelling on it. \n\nI hope that's a good sign? I really don't want to make it sound worse than it is, and I want to make sure I'm giving you the accurate picture. But no, I haven't noticed it happening just completely on its own without something reminding me first. Is that... does that make sense? I hope I'm answering that right.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way since the accident?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess it's been quite a while now. I hope I'm not taking up too much of your time by still struggling with this. \n\nIt started right after the accident, you know, the one with the machinery after they switched our schedules to overnight. And, um... it's been about two years and four months since then. I really thought I would have just gotten over it by now, and I feel a little bad that I haven't. I've tried my best to just push through and be a good employee, but it just seems to linger. Is it normal for it to take this long? I hope you don't think I'm just not trying hard enough to move on.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any problems in your relationships with family or friends since the accident?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess things have been a little harder lately, but I try really hard not to let it affect anyone else. I don't want to be a burden on my friends or my family, you know? \n\nI have... well, I've pulled back a bit from some things. I used to do community theater, which I loved, but... I just couldn't reliably get through the rehearsals anymore without needing to leave or take a break. So I stepped away, just so I wouldn't let the cast down. \n\nAnd at work... I hope this doesn't make me sound like a terrible employee, because I really do try my best... but I've missed quite a few shifts. And I guess I've lost my patience a few times with the new trainees. It's not their fault, I know that, but... they don't really trust me to mentor the younger inspectors anymore. \n\nI feel awful about it. I really want to be helpful and do a good job. I hope you don't think I'm just being difficult or lazy. Is it okay that I'm telling you all this? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or responsible in any way for the accident that happened at work?\n\nPatient: Oh... um... well, I know they said it was a mechanical issue, and I really try not to make it all about me because he's the one who actually suffered, but... yes. I guess maybe a little bit? I don't want to make it sound like I'm going crazy or anything, because I'm really not. But... sometimes, if I hear a loud clank from one of the carts on the floor, or... or if I catch the smell of hot oil, it's just... \n\nIt's like I'm right back in that bay. I just see my coworker by the machine, and it feels like it's happening right in front of me all over again. It's just for a second, really. I usually shake it off pretty quick. Is that... is that normal? I mean, for someone who went through something upsetting? I just hope I'm not making too big a deal out of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding any places, people, or activities since the incident?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I suppose I have been changing my routine just a tiny bit. I hope that's alright to admit. I don't want to sound like I'm not doing my job or anything, but... I do kind of go out of my way now so I don't have to walk past the inspection bay. It just takes an extra minute or two to walk the long way around, so it's not a huge problem for anyone else, I promise. \n\nAnd, um, I really don't talk about the accident if I can help it. If people bring it up, I just try to quietly change the subject. I don't want to make anyone else uncomfortable, you know? \n\nOh, and... well, this is a little embarrassing, but I actually quit going to these local industrial-history talks that I used to really enjoy. It sounds so silly, but the pictures they show of the old machinery... they just kind of set me off now. I feel bad for missing them, but it's just easier to stay home. Is it okay that I'm avoiding those things? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information, doctor.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself being constantly on the lookout for potential dangers or threats, even when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Well... I guess I do, a little bit. I mean, I try not to let it show, because I don't want people to think I'm paranoid or anything like that. But... even when I'm just in the break room, or, um, I sing in a choir and even at rehearsal, I just... \n\nI find myself watching the doors a lot. And I'm always listening, you know? Like, waiting for a sudden noise, or checking to see who's walking up behind me. It's silly, I know I'm perfectly safe in those places, but it's like... my body just won't believe it? \n\nI really try to keep it to myself so I don't bother anyone else with it. I hope that makes sense. Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're reliving the accident, like it's happening all over again, even when you're not thinking about it?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't think so. I mean, not really? \n\nLike I mentioned earlier, it really only happens if something sets it off, like that smell of the oil or a loud clank. But if I'm just sitting at home, or... or doing the dishes, it doesn't just happen completely out of the blue. I try very hard to keep my mind busy so I'm not just dwelling on it. \n\nI hope that's a good sign? I really don't want to make it sound worse than it is, and I want to make sure I'm giving you the accurate picture. But no, I haven't noticed it happening just completely on its own without something reminding me first. Is that... does that make sense? I hope I'm answering that right.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way since the accident?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess it's been quite a while now. I hope I'm not taking up too much of your time by still struggling with this. \n\nIt started right after the accident, you know, the one with the machinery after they switched our schedules to overnight. And, um... it's been about two years and four months since then. I really thought I would have just gotten over it by now, and I feel a little bad that I haven't. I've tried my best to just push through and be a good employee, but it just seems to linger. Is it normal for it to take this long? I hope you don't think I'm just not trying hard enough to move on.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any problems in your relationships with family or friends since the accident?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess things have been a little harder lately, but I try really hard not to let it affect anyone else. I don't want to be a burden on my friends or my family, you know? \n\nI have... well, I've pulled back a bit from some things. I used to do community theater, which I loved, but... I just couldn't reliably get through the rehearsals anymore without needing to leave or take a break. So I stepped away, just so I wouldn't let the cast down. \n\nAnd at work... I hope this doesn't make me sound like a terrible employee, because I really do try my best... but I've missed quite a few shifts. And I guess I've lost my patience a few times with the new trainees. It's not their fault, I know that, but... they don't really trust me to mentor the younger inspectors anymore. \n\nI feel awful about it. I really want to be helpful and do a good job. I hope you don't think I'm just being difficult or lazy. Is it okay that I'm telling you all this? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or responsible in any way for the accident that happened at work?\n\nPatient: Oh... um... well, I know they said it was a mechanical issue, and I really try not to make it all about me because he's the one who actually suffered, but... yes. I do. \n\nI just... I keep thinking that if I had pushed harder about the safety checks before we started, or... or if I had just stopped that run when it didn’t feel quite right to me... maybe he wouldn’t have gotten hurt so bad. I'm the inspector, you know? It's my job to catch those things, and I just feel so terrible that I didn't. \n\nI hope you don't think I'm being silly or just feeling sorry for myself. I really don't want to sound like a martyr or anything. But I just can't shake the feeling that I should have done more. Is it... is it normal to feel like I could have prevented it? I hope I'm explaining this okay, I just want to make sure I'm giving you the honest answer.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I wouldn't say I've lost interest. I hope I didn't give you the wrong impression earlier when I mentioned stepping away from the theater. I still really love it, and I still enjoy my other little hobbies, like tending to my garden and reading. \n\nIt's just... sometimes it's a bit harder to actually get out and do them right now, but the interest is definitely still there. I try very hard to keep up with the things I like because I know I shouldn't just sit around, and I really want to get back to normal. I haven't given up on my interests or anything like that, I promise. \n\nDoes that make sense? I just want to make sure I'm explaining it clearly so I'm giving you the right picture.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to find very enjoyable, but now find you have little to no interest in doing?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, like I was just mentioning, I really haven't lost my interest in the things I love. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information and not being confusing.\n\nDoctor: Are there any specific memories or reminders of the accident that you try to avoid thinking about?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... um... Oh, um... well, I still try to keep busy, I really do. I don't want to just sit around and do nothing. But... I guess maybe I haven't been as interested in things lately?

I hope you don't think I'm just giving up on everything. Is it normal to lose interest like that for a little while? I'm hoping it'll pass soon.

Oh, goodness, no... I don't think I'm a bad person. I really try my best to be a good person and do the right thing, I promise. I would never want to be a bad person.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, um... well, I don't want to sound like I'm throwing a pity party or anything. I really try not to dwell on it in a way that's... you know, unhelpful. 

But... yes, sometimes I do wonder if... if maybe I could have done more. I was the inspector, after all. That was my whole job. And I keep thinking... if I had just pushed a little harder about the safety checks that day, or... or if I had just stopped that run when things didn't quite feel right... maybe he wouldn't have gotten hurt so badly. 

I know I shouldn't beat myself up, and I really try to remind myself that I was just doing what I was told. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around hating myself all day, I promise I'm not. But it's hard not to feel responsible when your whole career is about catching mistakes, you know? Does that make sense? I hope I don't sound too... too negative. I'm really trying to be objective about it.

And, um... it's been about two years and four months since then. I really thought I would have just gotten over it by now, and I feel a little bad that I haven't. I've tried my best to just push through and be a good employee, but it just seems to linger. Is it normal for it to take this long? I hope you don't think I'm just not trying hard enough to move on.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any problems in your relationships with family or friends since the accident?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I just couldn't reliably get through the rehearsals anymore without needing to leave or take a break. So I stepped away, just so I wouldn't let the cast down. \n\nAnd at work... I hope this doesn't make me sound like a terrible employee, because I really do try my best... but I've missed quite a few shifts. And I guess I've lost my patience a few times with the new trainees. It's not their fault, I know that, but... they don't really trust me to mentor the younger inspectors anymore. \n\nI feel awful about it. I really want to be helpful and do a good job. I hope you don't think I'm just being difficult or lazy. Is it okay that I'm telling you all this? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or responsible in any way for the accident that happened at work?\n\nPatient: Oh... um... well, I know they said it was a mechanical issue, and I really try not to make it all about me because he's the one who actually suffered, but... yes. I really don't want to sound like a martyr or anything. But I just can't shake the feeling that I should have done more. Is it... is it normal to feel like I could have prevented it? I guess I do, a little bit. I mean, I try not to let it show, because I don't want people to think I'm paranoid or anything like that. But... even when I'm just in the break room, or, um, I sing in a choir and even at rehearsal, I just... \n\nI find myself watching the doors a lot. And I'm always listening, you know? Like, waiting for a sudden noise, or checking to see who's walking up behind me. It's silly, I know I'm perfectly safe in those places, but it's like... my body just won't believe it? \n\nI really try to keep it to myself so I don't bother anyone else with it. I hope that makes sense. Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're reliving the accident, like it's happening all over again, even when you're not thinking about it?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't think so. I mean, not really? \n\nLike I mentioned earlier, it really only happens if something sets it off, like that smell of the oil or a loud clank. But if I'm just sitting at home, or... or doing the dishes, it doesn't just happen completely out of the blue. I try very hard to keep my mind busy so I'm not just dwelling on it. \n\nI hope that's a good sign? I really don't want to make it sound worse than it is, and I want to make sure I'm giving you the accurate picture. But no, I haven't noticed it happening just completely on its own without something reminding me first. Is that... does that make sense? I hope I'm answering that right.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way since the accident?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess it's been quite a while now. I hope I'm not taking up too much of your time by still struggling with this. \n\nIt started right after the accident, you know, the one with the machinery after they switched our schedules to overnight. And, um... it's been about two years and four months since then. I really thought I would have just gotten over it by now, and I feel a little bad that I haven't. I've tried my best to just push through and be a good employee, but it just seems to linger. Is it normal for it to take this long? I hope you don't think I'm just not trying hard enough to move on.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any problems in your relationships with family or friends since the accident?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess things have been a little harder lately, but I try really hard not to let it affect anyone else. I don't want to be a burden on my friends or my family, you know? \n\nI have... well, I've pulled back a bit from some things. I used to do community theater, which I loved, but... I just couldn't reliably get through the rehearsals anymore without needing to leave or take a break. So I stepped away, just so I wouldn't let the cast down. \n\nAnd at work... I hope this doesn't make me sound like a terrible employee, because I really do try my best... but I've missed quite a few shifts. And I guess I've lost my patience a few times with the new trainees. It's not their fault, I know that, but... they don't really trust me to mentor the younger inspectors anymore. \n\nI feel awful about it. I really want to be helpful and do a good job. I hope you don't think I'm just being difficult or lazy. Is it okay that I'm telling you all this? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or responsible in any way for the accident that happened at work?\n\nPatient: Oh... um... well, I know they said it was a mechanical issue, and I really try not to make it all about me because he's the one who actually suffered, but... yes. I do. \n\nI just... I keep thinking that if I had pushed harder about the safety checks before we started, or... or if I had just stopped that run when it didn’t feel quite right to me... maybe he wouldn’t have gotten hurt so bad. I'm the inspector, you know? It's my job to catch those things, and I just feel so terrible that I didn't. \n\nI hope you don't think I'm being silly or just feeling sorry for myself. I really don't want to sound like a martyr or anything. But I just can't shake the feeling that I should have done more. Is it... is it normal to feel like I could have prevented it? I hope I'm explaining this okay, I just want to make sure I'm giving you the honest answer.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I wouldn't say I've lost interest. I hope I didn't give you the wrong impression earlier when I mentioned stepping away from the theater. I still really love it, and I still enjoy my other little hobbies, like tending to my garden and reading. \n\nIt's just... sometimes it's a bit harder to actually get out and do them right now, but the interest is definitely still there. I try very hard to keep up with the things I like because I know I shouldn't just sit around, and I really want to get back to normal. I haven't given up on my interests or anything like that, I promise. \n\nDoes that make sense? I just want to make sure I'm explaining it clearly so I'm giving you the right picture.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to find very enjoyable, but now find you have little to no interest in doing?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, like I was just mentioning, I really haven't lost my interest in the things I love. I'm sorry if I didn't explain it well enough a second ago! I still find my gardening and my books very enjoyable... in my heart, I mean. \n\nEven if I can't always get out and do them as easily right now, I still care about them just as much as I used to. I really try my hardest to stay positive and keep up with my life, I promise. I don't want you to think I've just given up. \n\nI hope that makes sense? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information and not being confusing.\n\nDoctor: Are there any specific memories or reminders of the accident that you try to avoid thinking about?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... um... I try my best to face things, I really do, but... yes, I suppose there are a few things. I hope it doesn't make me sound like I'm just running away from my problems. But at work, I... I kind of go out of my way not to pass the inspection bay anymore. I just take a slightly different route, you know? And I really won't talk about the accident if I can help it. I don't want to make anyone uncomfortable or bring down the mood. \n\nOh, and... well, I actually stopped going to those industrial-history talks I used to enjoy so much. I know I should probably just push through it, and I promise I'm trying to get better about it, but... is it okay that I'm avoiding those things for now? I hope I'm giving you the right kind of examples, I just want to make sure I'm answering you properly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant difficulties in your daily life or work due to these feelings or experiences?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I really try not to let it affect my work, I promise I do. I want to be a good employee and a good person to be around. But... I suppose it has been a little difficult lately. I hope you don't think less of me, but... I've missed a few shifts. Well, maybe more than a few. \n\nAnd, um, I used to mentor the younger inspectors, which I loved, but... I guess I've lost my patience a bit with the trainees lately. They don't really trust me to do it right now, which is completely understandable, I know I haven't been myself. \n\nAnd I mentioned the theater earlier... I actually had to pull back from that because I just couldn't reliably get through the rehearsals without, you know, needing to leave or getting upset. I feel terrible about letting people down. I'm really trying to get back on track, I don't want to be a burden to anyone. Does that... does that answer your question? I hope I'm not sounding too negative, I just want to be honest with you.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're detached from yourself or the world around you?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't think so. I mean, I know sometimes people talk about feeling like they're in a dream or something, but... no, I'm very much here. I try to stay really grounded, you know? \n\nI know I get upset sometimes, like I said, but I always know who I am and where I am. I hope that's a good thing? I just want to make sure I'm not supposed to be feeling that way... I really try to stay present and focused on what I need to do, I promise. Is that the kind of answer you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid expressing your emotions or talking about your feelings to others?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I really try to be open, I do. I don't want to burden anyone, you know? Everyone has their own things going on, and I'd hate to be the person who's always complaining or bringing down the mood. I try to just keep a smile on my face and handle things myself so I'm not a bother. I hope that doesn't make me sound stubborn... I just want to be pleasant to be around. Is it okay that I keep things to myself a bit? I'm trying to be strong, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now find that you don't enjoy as much as you used to?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I still try to keep busy, I really do. I'm hoping it'll pass soon.\n\nDoctor: Do you often think that you're a bad person or that you're worthless because of what happened?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no... I don't think I'm a bad person. I don't want to sound bitter or resentful, you know? I'm usually not the type to point fingers. I hope you don't think poorly of me for having those kinds of thoughts... is it normal to feel a little useless like that after something bad happens? I'm really trying to work past it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often think that you're to blame for the accident and that it's a reflection of your worth as a person?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I don't want to sound like I'm throwing a pity party or anything. I really try not to dwell on it in a way that's... you know, unhelpful. \n\nBut... yes, sometimes I do wonder if... if maybe I could have done more. I was the inspector, after all. That was my whole job.
